No additional directly owned, verified GS-IV Section-B case through 2026 was traced beyond the fifty-four rows above. The canonical owners also catalogue probable questions on applying the architecture to disaster, corporate, procurement, communal, border, custodial, consent, predictive-policing, institutional-capture, welfare-technology, conflict-neutrality and export-decision scenarios; this session treats those as probable framings only, not as past papers.

# CUMULATIVE CONCEPT CHECKS

### Cumulative check A — Lessons 1-5

**Question:** A candidate reads a case in which a Collector must decide whether to permit a factory expansion that will create jobs but increase pollution in a densely populated ward, and immediately writes: "The Collector must balance development and environment. He should permit the expansion with strict conditions." Using Lessons 1 to 5, identify every element that is missing and show what each would have contributed.

**Model answer:** Five elements are missing and each would have changed the answer. Element 1 is absent because nothing separates what the stem verifies from what the candidate assumes — whether the pollution increase is established or projected, and whether the ward's exposure is measured or asserted, determine whether this is a decision on evidence or a decision under uncertainty. Element 2 is absent, so the silent stakeholders never appear: the residents who will not be consulted, and the future residents who inherit the exposure. Element 3 is absent, so the binding constraint is unidentified — whether the Collector has authority over the clearance at all, or whether his role is to recommend to a pollution control authority, which decides whether his output is an order or a recommendation. Element 4 is absent because "permit with conditions" is one option presented as a conclusion, with no comparison against refusal, against conditional phased permission tied to verified abatement, or against relocation to a less densely populated site. And because there is no option set, Element 5 has nothing to evaluate, so no check can be shown to be decisive and the word "balance" carries weight it cannot bear. The repair is to name the constraint first, generate three or four real options including a sequenced one, and let the evaluation choose between them rather than defend a conclusion already reached.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not mistake naming a tension for analysing it. "Balance development and environment" identifies the subject of the case rather than resolving it, and an answer that opens with a balance and closes with a condition has skipped every element that would have shown which balance, on what evidence, within whose authority.

### Cumulative check B — Lessons 6-9

**Question:** A candidate evaluating a case about a municipal engineer writes: "The six checks apply as follows: there is no conflict of interest; the action is legal; it serves the public interest; it is proportionate; it is transparent; and it is compassionate. Therefore the officer should proceed." Explain what has gone wrong, and show what a correct evaluation of the same facts would look like in structure.

**Model answer:** The candidate has used the checks as a checklist to be ticked rather than as an instrument for choosing between options, and three consequences follow. First, the checks have been applied to a single course of action rather than to a set, so nothing has been eliminated and nothing has been ranked — the evaluation confirms a conclusion instead of producing one. Second, the hard-threshold and weighted distinction has disappeared: conflict of interest and legality should have been applied first to eliminate options, and the remaining four to rank the survivors, with one named as decisive. Third, where every check is satisfied, the case is not ethically difficult, which almost certainly means the candidate has not found the real tension — in most GS-IV cases at least two weighted checks pull against each other, and an evaluation in which all six agree usually indicates that the option set was too narrow. A correct evaluation applies conflict of interest and legality to the three or four generated options and states which are thereby eliminated; applies public interest, proportionality, transparency and compassion to what survives; names the genuine tension between at least two of them; and states which check is decisive and argues that weighting from the facts rather than asserting it.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not run the six checks against your preferred option. Checks applied after a decision has been made can only confirm it, and an evaluation in which every check is satisfied and no tension appears is evidence that the option set was too narrow rather than that the case was easy.

### Cumulative check C — Lessons 10-13

**Question:** An officer facing improper pressure writes an answer that ends: "I would refuse to comply, and if the pressure continued I would resign, as that is the only honourable course." Assess this conclusion using Lessons 10 to 13, and state what the answer should have contained instead.

**Model answer:** The conclusion fails three of the four lessons. On Element 6, "refuse to comply" is not an executable decision — it names an attitude rather than an act, and it addresses no objection, so the examiner cannot see what the officer actually does on the file or in the office the next morning. On Element 7, there is no implementation or communication at all: no record of the instruction, no reasoned position stated, no referral, and nothing said to seniors, team or affected parties, which means the refusal exists only in two people's memories and leaves the same pressure available to use on a successor. On Element 8, there is no residual risk, no monitoring mechanism and no named owner, so the answer claims a completeness it cannot have. And on the resignation test, the conclusion is asserted rather than evaluated: it does not establish that lawful internal options were exhausted, it does not ask whether resigning would improve the public-interest outcome or merely remove a principled voice, and it does not consider whether staying with recorded dissent would be the more effective safeguard. The answer should instead have recorded the instruction in a file note or sought written confirmation, recorded the officer's own reasoned position on the same file, escalated through the appropriate channel if the pressure persisted, continued performing the substantive duty meanwhile, and named the specific contingency — being compelled to personally execute the act despite recorded dissent — on which resignation would become correct.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not treat resignation as a conclusion that needs no justification because it sounds principled. In most administrative fact patterns resignation removes the only person objecting while the decision proceeds unchanged, which helps the wrongdoer; the test requires the case for it to be argued from the facts, and where the answer is to stay, the contingency that would change that must still be named.

### Cumulative check D — Lessons 14-17

**Question:** A single case presents four features: a scheme whose enrolment consent was taken verbally from poorly literate applicants; an automated eligibility check that rejects a predictable group; a front-line office in which intermediaries have captured the process; and a limited fund that cannot pay every valid claim. Identify which template applies to each feature and name the one question each template would force the answer to confront.

**Model answer:** Each feature maps onto a different template and each template forces a distinct question. The verbal enrolment consent engages the consent-validity test, and its forcing question is the adequate-information step: was the nature, purpose, material consequences and the effect of refusal actually communicated in a language and form these applicants understood, which a simplified verbal explanation to poorly literate participants almost certainly fails — with the scope step close behind, since consent given for enrolment may not reach whatever the scheme later does with the data. The automated rejection engages the automated adverse-decision template, and its forcing question is record access: can the rejected person see what the system holds against her, because if she cannot she can neither correct an error nor substantiate a complaint, and the absence of proven harm then reflects the opacity rather than the system's accuracy. The captured front office engages the two-track template, and its forcing question is simultaneity: what will keep the lawful service flowing to the people the capture is harming while the evidence is being built confidentially, since a single-track answer will either paralyse the office or leave the network intact. The limited fund engages triage and criteria-based denial, and its forcing question is whether the criteria were fixed before the claimants were known, because criteria written after they appear are indistinguishable from rationalisation.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not look for a single template that will answer a composite case. Real stems frequently contain two or three template-shaped problems at once, and the skill is recognising which feature engages which template rather than forcing the whole case into the one you practised most recently.

### Cumulative check E — Lessons 18-22

**Question:** Across the disaster, financial-propriety, public-money, political-pressure and public-order families, identify the single structural move that recurs in the correct answer to each, and explain why the same move works across such different facts.

**Model answer:** The recurring move is refusing the binary the stem appears to offer, and constructing a third option that discharges the competing obligation differently rather than abandoning it. In the disaster case it is delegation — the public duty is discharged through a competent, briefed, written and monitored arrangement rather than by choosing between post and family. In the financial-propriety case it is the technically independent emergency scope — urgency is served within delegated power while the full estimate goes upward, rather than choosing between an unlawful split and a dangerous delay. In the public-money case it is the recorded objection with escalation — propriety is pursued without either silent compliance or a resignation that removes the only objector. In the political-pressure case it is routing the same material through the official channel — accountability is pursued without the partisan act that would cost the officer his neutrality. In the public-order case it is the scaled-down, rerouted, time-bound assembly or the sequenced package — order is protected without a blanket restriction that forfeits the legitimacy the eventual settlement needs. The move works across such different facts because the binary in each stem is produced not by the ethics but by the framing: both poles are visible and easy, so a candidate under time pressure sees only them, while the third option requires identifying which constraint actually binds and designing around it. That is why Element 3 and Element 4 carry so much of the architecture's weight.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not assume that a third option always exists and manufacture one where the facts close it. Where a production constraint permits only one order, or where a clinical choice is genuinely between sampling and removing, the hybrid is unavailable and the answer must make a real allocation or a real choice — the skill is testing for the third option, not assuming it.

### Cumulative check F — Lessons 23-28

**Question:** A candidate has mastered the method but keeps losing marks on subpart (b). Across the consent, due-process, border, technology and transfer families, state what subpart (b) most often demands, and give the one discipline that would fix the problem in each.

**Model answer:** Across these families subpart (b) almost never asks for more of subpart (a); it asks for a judgement of a different kind, and the discipline is to identify which kind before writing. In the consent family it demands an appraisal of a person's conduct, so the discipline is to separate motive from the rightness of the act from conduct after the fact, and to deliver a balanced verdict rather than a restatement of the violation. In the due-process family it demands that the answer hold both directions at once, so the discipline is to show how the same mechanism protects complainant and respondent and why failing either destroys the other. In the border family it demands the separation principle rather than a uniform response, so the discipline is to ask whether the legally relevant status is uniform across the group and to design streams where it is not. In the technology family it demands a normative thesis about a general practice rather than a description of the instance, so the discipline is to state the thesis, give three grounds, and offer an operative test the examiner can apply to other cases. In the transfer family it demands a thesis on the relation between two interests that the stem has framed as opposed, so the discipline is to refuse the false opposition and show the interests are time-horizon-dependent, while rebutting the replaceability argument planted in the stem. The common fix is to read the subpart's own verb first and budget for it separately.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not treat subpart (b) as the place to finish subpart (a). In the verified 2026 bank the two subparts carried consistently different demands, and the most expensive single error in Section B is spending the second half of the word budget restating the first half in different words.

# ORIGINAL 10-, 15- AND 20-MARK MAINS MODEL PRACTICE

### 10-mark model practice

**Question:** Distinguish a hard-threshold check from a weighted check in case-study evaluation, and explain why the distinction, rather than the list of checks itself, determines the quality of an answer. Answer in 150 words.

**Model answer:**
A **hard threshold** eliminates an option outright; a **weighted check** ranks the options that survive. Conflict of interest and legality are hard thresholds: an illegal or self-interested option is struck out, not placed on a scale against its benefits. Public interest, proportionality, transparency and compassion are weighted, and they genuinely conflict — in a triage case aggregate welfare may favour one allocation while priority to the worst-off favours another.

The distinction determines answer quality because the list alone produces a checklist applied to a preferred option, which can only confirm it. The distinction produces a **sequence**: thresholds first, so options are eliminated; weighted checks next, on survivors only; then an explicit statement of which weighted check was decisive, argued from the facts.

It also prevents the commonest error — "proceeding carefully despite a conflict", which treats a threshold as a factor to be outweighed.

**Why this scores:** It states the operational difference in one line each, supplies a concrete instance of genuine conflict between weighted checks, converts the distinction into an examinable sequence rather than leaving it abstract, and names the specific error the distinction prevents.

### 15-mark model practice

**Question:** "A case-study answer is complete once the right decision has been identified and justified." Critically examine this claim with reference to the elements that follow the decision, and explain what each contributes that the decision itself cannot. Answer in 250 words.

**Model answer:**
The claim should be rejected, though its premise — that a reasoned decision is the answer's centre — is correct and worth crediting first.

Three things remain afterwards, and each supplies what the decision cannot.

**Implementation** converts an opinion into an administrative act. "The officer should act with integrity" is unexecutable; "a written office order the same day specifying the disposal sequence, the acknowledgment system and a weekly signed report" can be carried out by someone else tomorrow. The test is whether a reader could execute it without a further question; a decision alone rarely passes.

**Communication** is not presentation but, in several case families, the operative remedy. Where wrongdoing is rent on opacity and delay, publishing the queue, the criteria and the timeline destroys that rent before any charge-sheet. Three audiences also differ: seniors need authorisation on record, the team unambiguous authority, affected parties a stated timeline.

**Residual-risk mitigation** supplies honesty. Every administrative decision leaves something unresolved — the remedy's own characteristic risk, a risk the decision cannot reach, or the risk of recurrence. Naming it with a monitoring mechanism and an accountable office, assigned away from whoever has an incentive to find nothing, distinguishes an administrator from an advocate.

A qualified conclusion: identifying and justifying the decision is necessary and insufficient. An answer that stops there has described a judgement rather than made a decision.

**Why this scores:** It concedes the premise before rejecting the claim, gives each post-decision element a distinct function rather than listing them, supplies the executable-by-a-stranger test and the opacity-rent insight as concrete analytical content, and closes with a precisely qualified verdict rather than a flat denial.

### 20-mark model practice

**Question:** "Structured case-study architectures produce formulaic answers; genuine ethical judgement cannot be reduced to a sequence of elements and checks." Critically examine this statement with reference to how the architecture actually operates, its genuine limitations, and what it does and does not claim. Answer in 250 words.

**Model answer:**
The statement contains a real insight and draws the wrong conclusion from it.

Its insight is that the framework is **not an algorithm**. It is a synthesis heuristic: different well-reasoned weightings can defensibly favour different decisions in hard cases, and what is assessed is reasoning quality, not conformity to one answer. No official model answer exists, so no architecture can honestly claim to produce one.

But the conclusion fails on three grounds.

First, the architecture supplies **questions, not answers**. It asks what is given rather than assumed, who is affected including the voiceless, what bounds the protagonist's authority, what the real alternatives are, and which checks eliminate rather than rank. Two candidates using it on the same case write recognisably different answers if they read the facts differently.

Second, what examiners penalise is **content-free structure** — eight headings with a line under each. That is a failure of execution, not of design, and its remedy is more analysis within the elements, not fewer elements.

Third, the alternative is worse. Unstructured judgement under time pressure reliably produces the binary the stem suggests, the heroic option that ignores constraints, and a conclusion nobody can audit, including the candidate.

Three genuine limitations should be conceded: over-decomposition dilutes focus, overdone steelmanning reads as indecision, and the framework cannot resolve a value conflict it can only make visible.

A qualified verdict: the architecture disciplines judgement rather than replacing it, and earns marks precisely where it makes reasoning auditable.

**Why this scores:** It credits the statement's genuine insight and states the framework's own honest self-description before answering, separates a failure of execution from a failure of design, supplies the unstructured-alternative comparison that the statement omits, concedes three real limitations by name, and closes with a qualified verdict rather than a defence.

# REMEDIATION

| Common weak answer move | Why it loses marks | Repair move |
|---|---|---|
| Answering subpart (b) by restating subpart (a) | In 2024-2026 every Section-B case carried two subparts with different demands; this forfeits roughly half the credit on that case | Read the subpart's own verb first and budget roughly 120-130 words for each demand separately (Lesson 1) |
| Writing 250 words on the first subpart | The word instruction applies to the whole case, both subparts together | Budget before writing; where (b) is a thesis, rebudget to thesis, three grounds, operative test, qualification (Lessons 1, 28) |
| Narrative prose with no visible decision points | There is nothing for the examiner to credit — no stakeholder, constraint, option, check or decision identified | Signpost the elements; name rather than describe (Lessons 2, 28) |
| Eight headings with one line under each | Structure without analysis; this is what examiners actually penalise as formulaic | Compress facts and stakeholders to clauses and spend the words on evaluation, decision and implementation (Lesson 2) |
| Upgrading an allegation into a finding, or "could be" into "is" | Answers a case the examiner did not set, and usually produces an evidence-free punitive recommendation | Keep two columns — given facts and assumptions — and flag any assumption explicitly (Lesson 3) |
| Listing stakeholders as a roll-call of everyone named in the stem | Stakeholders who never reappear in the evaluation are decoration | Name decisively, including the vulnerable sub-group and one traceable silent stakeholder (Lesson 3) |
| Recording the difficulty of the situation as a constraint | An entrenched problem or a powerful interest group is something the option must deal with, not a boundary on authority | Use the five categories — legal and delegation, time, information, resource, jurisdiction — and name the one that binds (Lesson 4) |
| Offering a binary as the option set | A fork is not a design; it conceals the hybrid, sequenced, parallel and conditional options that usually carry the answer | Generate three to four, each with one concrete pro and one concrete con (Lesson 5) |
| Running the six checks as a checklist against a preferred option | Checks applied after a decision can only confirm it, and an evaluation where all six agree means the option set was too narrow | Hard thresholds first to eliminate, weighted checks on survivors, then name and argue the decisive one (Lesson 6) |
| Proceeding "carefully" despite a genuine conflict of interest | Treats a hard threshold as a factor to be outweighed; the problem is that nobody can afterwards verify the decision was clean | Disclose in writing, recuse from that matter, route to an untainted authority, decide on criteria fixed in advance (Lesson 8) |
| Citing the decision-maker's good character or an employer's trust as a safeguard | A safeguard satisfied by the subject's own confidence is not a safeguard, and declared trust removes an external check rather than supplying one | Name that reasoning as the reasoning a compromised outcome would use (Lesson 8) |
| Reciting the three moral theories in three sentences that reach the same conclusion | The theories do no independent work and could be transplanted into any case | Use each as a lens asking a different question; where they converge say so briefly, and where they diverge make the divergence the ethical issue (Lesson 7) |
| Decomposing a single-issue case into four sub-dilemmas | Restatements of one tension in different vocabulary waste the word budget | Apply the separability test — could it arise independently, and is a different check decisive? (Lesson 9) |
| Constructing a weak objection and defeating it in one line | A strawman leaves the real objection standing in the examiner's mind | Find the practical objection — will it work, how fast, at what cost — and either answer it in two lines or build it in as a design constraint (Lesson 10) |
| Answering improper oral pressure with a firm verbal refusal | Leaves no trace, becomes one word against another, and moves the pressure to a successor | Record the instruction, record your reasoned position, refer up, continue the substantive duty meanwhile (Lesson 11) |
| Ending with "the problem is thus resolved" | Claims a completeness no administrative decision has | Close with the residual risk, its monitoring mechanism and the named office accountable, assigned away from whoever has an incentive to find nothing (Lesson 12) |
| Reaching for resignation as the honourable conclusion | Usually removes the only objector while the decision proceeds unchanged | Run the three-question test, and where the answer is to stay, name the contingency that would change it (Lesson 13) |
| Jumping to public disclosure because one channel is blocked | The compromised-rung rule removes the captured rung, not the whole ladder | Identify the next genuinely independent tier and the evidence needed to reach it (Lessons 13, 20) |
| Treating a relative's written consent as consent for a competent adult | Documentation does not supply validity; a signature from someone with no right to sign is a record | Apply the seven-step test, with authority and scope stated separately (Lessons 14, 23) |
| Invoking the emergency exception on one limb | The exception requires both an immediate threat to life or health and unreasonableness in waiting | State both limbs and show which fails (Lessons 14, 23) |
| Offering front-line discretion as the humane fix for a failing automated gate | Privatises an entitlement decision to an interested party and creates an unaccountable rent | Structure the exception — numbered, recorded, countersigned, rate-capped and audited (Lessons 15, 26) |
| Treating "no proof of bias" as a reason for inaction | Where the affected cannot see their own data, absence of proof reflects the opacity complained of | State the burden-of-proof point explicitly and make record access a remedy (Lessons 15, 26) |
| Recommending mass suspension to clean up a captured office | Evidence-thin, likely to collapse, and it halts the office, punishing the applicants | Run the continuity and accountability tracks simultaneously, with action confined to those against whom material exists (Lessons 16, 20) |
| Abandoning a statutory mechanism because one office-holder in it is conflicted | Forfeits the timelines, confidentiality and anti-retaliation protection that mechanism carries | Repair the specific defect — recusal and reconstitution — while keeping the lawful process running (Lessons 16, 24) |
| Refusing to publish allocation criteria in the name of compassion | Unpublished discretion advantages whoever has the best access to the decision-maker | Publish criteria in advance, state which principle is prioritised, record what was applied, review as the situation develops (Lesson 17) |
| Writing criteria after the claimants are known | Indistinguishable from rationalisation, however good the criteria are | Fix criteria before claimants are known and document claimant-by-claimant against each (Lesson 17) |
| Weighing a threshold screen against a commercial term | A screen weighed against revenue has ceased to be a screen | Run the screens first; compare only the survivors commercially (Lessons 17, 27) |
| Treating equal totals as proof of impartiality in a conflict case | Identical totals to unequal populations is discrimination produced by a gesture of even-handedness | Publish equal per-capita norms and let the totals differ visibly because the rule produced them (Lesson 22) |
| Applying one uniform response to a mixed group | Both blanket entry and blanket pushback ignore legally relevant differences within the group | Ask whether the legally relevant status is uniform, and design streams where it is not (Lesson 25) |
| Asserting a refugee-status process, or resolving the non-refoulement question | India is not a party to the Convention or Protocol and has no dedicated refugee statute; the customary-law question remains open | Ground the civilians' entitlement in the due-process and life-and-liberty guarantees that apply to every person on Indian soil (Lesson 25) |
| Treating an interim order declining a stay as a final holding | Misstates the law and converts a strong argument into a factual error | State the order's interim character and what it left open (Lesson 25) |
| Accepting "someone else will supply it anyway" | Complicity is not excused by substitutability, and the argument proves too much | Rebut on all four grounds — moral, practical, risk and leverage (Lesson 27) |
| Quoting a section number for a renumbered or superseded provision | A wrong citation converts a good argument into a factual error | Cite the principle and name the instrument; say the provision should be verified (Lesson 28) |

# MASTER COMPARISON, CAUSAL AND ARGUMENT MAPS

### Master comparison table — which template answers which case type

| Case signature in the stem | Template | The question it forces | Worked families |
|---|---|---|---|
| Someone agreed, or someone else agreed for them | Consent-validity test | Was the agreement competent, authorised, voluntary, informed, in scope, and is the emergency exception made out on **both** limbs? | Therapeutic consent; research enrolment; database repurposing; land and community consent |
| A system, device, algorithm or rule produced an adverse outcome | Automated adverse-decision template | Can the affected person see what is held against her, and is there a usable cascade for the known failure population? | Biometric welfare exclusion; predictive-policing flags; eligibility rejection; database mismatch |
| An institution the protagonist depends on is itself captured or broken | Parallel clean-up-without-shutting-down | What keeps the lawful service flowing to the people the wrongdoing is harming, **while** evidence is built confidentially? | Tout network; compromised conflict-district administration; captured complaints committee; a failing scheme |
| One claimant must be preferred, or a request refused, or there is not enough for everyone | Triage and criteria-based denial | Were the criteria fixed before the claimants were known, and did the screens run before the commercial comparison? | Scarce-resource triage; export allocation; compensation; tender award; licensing |

### Master causal chain — from a printed stem to a marked answer

```text
 PRINTED STEM + TWO SUBPARTS WITH DIFFERENT DEMANDS
        |
        v
 READ THE SUBPART VERBS FIRST -> decides the budget and the output type
        |      (issues? options? a person's conduct? a normative thesis?)
        v
 SEPARATE FACTS FROM ASSUMPTIONS -> decides WHICH case you are answering
        |
        v
 MAP STAKEHOLDERS INCLUDING THE SILENT -> reveals the PRECEDENT, which is
        |                                  what prices the easy options
        v
 NAME THE BINDING CONSTRAINT -> deletes the heroic option and GENERATES
        |                       the shape of the right one
        v
 RECOGNISE THE CASE TYPE -> if a template fits, it supplies the questions
        |                   this case type always requires
        v
 GENERATE 3-4 OPTIONS -> poles + hybrid / sequenced / parallel / conditional
        |
        v
 HARD THRESHOLDS ELIMINATE  ->  WEIGHTED CHECKS RANK  ->  NAME THE DECISIVE ONE
        |                        (and find the real tension; if all six agree,
        |                         the option set was too narrow)
        v
 DECIDE -> named, executable, with the STRONGEST objection answered or built in
        |
        v
 IMPLEMENT AND COMMUNICATE -> who, what, by when, recorded how;
        |                      seniors / team / affected parties
        |                      (and in capture cases, the communication IS the remedy)
        v
 CLOSE -> residual risk + monitoring mechanism + NAMED accountable office
        |
        v
 AN ANSWER ANOTHER OFFICIAL COULD AUDIT
```

### Master argument map — why the architecture is disciplined judgement rather than a formula

```text
 CLAIM UNDER TEST: "A structured case-study architecture produces formulaic answers."

 EVIDENCE FOR                               EVIDENCE AGAINST
 - the elements recur in every answer        - the architecture supplies QUESTIONS; the
 - the vocabulary is shared across cases       answers must come from the specific facts
 - a weak user can produce eight headings    - two users reading the facts differently
   with nothing under them                     write recognisably different answers
 - over-decomposition and overdone           - what is penalised is CONTENT-FREE structure,
   steelmanning genuinely do damage            which is a failure of execution not design
   answers                                   - the unstructured alternative reliably
 - the framework cannot resolve a value        produces the stem's binary, the heroic
   conflict; it can only make it visible       option, and an unauditable conclusion
 - there is no official model answer, so     - the same structure is how a defensible
   no architecture can claim to produce it     administrative file note is written

                 \                                       /
                  \                                     /
                   v                                   v
 WHY NEITHER SIDE WINS OUTRIGHT:
 the two columns describe different objects. The left describes what a WEAK USER
 does with the architecture; the right describes what the architecture IS and what
 it claims about itself — a synthesis heuristic that disciplines reasoning, not an
 algorithm that outputs one correct answer.

                                      |
                                      v
 QUALIFIED CONCLUSION: the architecture is a reasoning discipline whose value lies in
 making judgement auditable. It does not determine the decision, it does not resolve
 genuine value conflicts, and it can be executed badly as empty scaffolding. What it
 reliably prevents is the three failures that cost most marks — answering a case you
 assumed rather than the one printed, recommending something the protagonist cannot do,
 and reaching a conclusion whose reasoning nobody, including the candidate, can check.
 QUALIFICATION: develop the elements the specific subpart's directive demands, and let
 the others appear as clauses; an architecture applied at uniform depth has misread the
 question it was meant to answer.
```

# COMPLETE CONSOLIDATED REGISTER NOTES

## 1. What Section B asks
- Audited 2024, 2025 and 2026: Section A Q1-Q6 carries 130 marks; Section B Q7-Q12 carries 120 as six 20-mark cases. The 2026 paper shows how Section A reaches 130 — Q3 carries three 10-mark subparts.
- Treat the 130:120 split as a three-year verified pattern, not a permanent guarantee.
- "(Answer in 250 words) 20" covers both subparts together — roughly 120-130 words each.
- Subpart conflation is the paper's most expensive structural error; five of the six 2026 (b) subparts demanded a decision or a thesis, Q7(b) demanded a conduct appraisal, four of the (a) subparts demanded issue identification, and Q10(a) and Q12(a) asked for options and a decision in the first subpart.
- No published official model answer exists; the architecture is a defensible synthesis and the quality of reasoning is what is marked.

## 2. The eight elements
- Facts; stakeholders; constraints; options; ethical evaluation; decision; implementation and communication; residual-risk mitigation.
- A dependency chain — each element produces the input the next needs.
- Constraints earn their own element because weak answers propose what the protagonist cannot do; the constraint generates the shape of the right option.
- Hard constraint deletes an option; soft constraint prices it.
- The elements govern what must be present, not how much space each takes.

## 3. Facts, value separation and the silent stakeholder
- Two columns: what the stem verifies, and what you assume or judge — and flag any assumption explicitly.
- Fatal upgrades: allegation into finding; "could be malignant" into "is"; fear into complicity; a few complaints into proven guilt.
- Stakeholder categories: direct actors; direct affected; indirect affected; the vulnerable sub-group inside a category; the silent stakeholder; future generations; the institution's own credibility.
- The silent stakeholder is usually the person on whom the **precedent** falls — and must be traceable to a printed fact.
- A stakeholder who never reappears in the evaluation was decoration.

## 4. Constraint mapping
- Five categories with their surfacing questions: legal and delegation; time; information; resource and capacity; jurisdiction.
- Time constraints matter for what becomes **irreversible**, not merely for urgency.
- The response to an information constraint is an option that resolves the uncertainty or is robust to both possibilities.
- A jurisdictional constraint separates what you **order** from what you **recommend upward**.
- Name the one binding constraint; test an asserted constraint when it comes from an interested party.
- Constraints bound the choice; concluding that nothing can be done misuses the element.

## 5. Generating options
- Five of twelve verified 2026 subparts expressly demand options or alternatives — Q8(b), Q9(b), Q10(a), Q11(b), Q12(a).
- Five sources: the two poles; the hybrid; the sequenced; the parallel; the conditional.
- Three to four options, each with one concrete pro and one concrete con; include and reject the poles with stated reasons.
- Sequence is itself an ethical judgement; the parallel option is the one most often missed.
- The hybrid is not automatically correct — a production constraint or a binary clinical choice can close it.

## 6. The six checks
- Conflict of interest; legality; public interest; proportionality; transparency; compassion and long-term consequence.
- Conflict of interest and legality are **hard thresholds** that eliminate; the other four are **weighted** and rank survivors.
- "Legality is almost always a hard threshold" covers propriety cases with no bright-line illegality; it does not license an unlawful choice.
- Weighted checks genuinely conflict — but test whether a tension is real, since correctly defining fiscal probity as preventing leakage dissolves one of them.
- Sequence: thresholds eliminate, weighted checks rank, then name and argue the decisive one.
- Where every surviving option is lawful, legality decides nothing and recognising that is a skill.

## 7. Moral theory and the decision ledger
- Three lenses asking different questions: duty and rights; who gains and loses, how much and over what horizon; what practical wisdom would do.
- Each catches what the others miss — rights surviving a good outcome; a harm small now and large later; the heroic gesture and the cowardly evasion.
- Convergence gets a clause; divergence **is** the ethical issue.
- Ledger: fact-value separation; stakeholders; precisely named ethical issues; legal and constitutional floor with constitutional morality as a public justification standard; legality versus ethics; options against consequence, duty, virtue, justice, care and public-interest tests with short and long-term effects separated; reasoned choice with the strongest counter-argument answered; implementation and preventive institutional reform.
- Compressed rule: legality is the floor not the ethical ceiling; compassion is a lens not a licence; a reasoned choice is incomplete without executable safeguards.

## 8. Conflict of interest
- Disclose in writing on the file → recuse from the specific decision → route to an untainted authority → decide on criteria fixed in advance.
- Each step answers the objection the previous leaves open; truncation relocates the suspicion.
- Disclosure must precede the decision, because an apparent conflict is about what was known when the decision was made.
- Recusal is surgical. Asking the relative to withdraw builds no durable safeguard.
- "My relative is trustworthy" and "management trusts me fully" are the reasoning a compromised outcome would use; declared trust removes an external check.
- Privileged information escalates from a conduct-rule breach to undue advantage if acted on.
- Where no unconflicted alternate exists: still disclose, bind the decision tightly to published criteria, and refer the choice of decider upward.

## 9. Nested-dilemma decomposition
- Identify separable sub-dilemmas; solve each with its own compact pass; integrate so the resolutions reinforce each other.
- Separability test: could it arise independently, **and** is a different check decisive in it?
- Integration is not optional; decompose proportionately, not maximally.
- A procurement conflict is one dilemma; a compromised-administration conflict case is genuinely three.
- Decomposition often maps onto the two subparts — issues in (a), resolution in (b).

## 10. Decision and steelmanning
- A decision names the option, the decisive check, why the rejected options fail, and the strongest objection answered.
- Executability test: could a reader carry it out without asking a further question?
- The strongest objection is usually practical, not moral — about whether the course will work.
- Convert the objection into a **design constraint**: time-bound and verifiable commitments; written, monitored delegation; parallel time-bound verification.
- One objection in two or three lines; relitigating reads as indecision.
- Read the subpart's own verb for the degree of commitment required.

## 11. Implementation, communication and the written record
- Who does what, by when, recorded how, reviewed when.
- Three audiences: seniors need authorisation on record; the team needs unambiguous authority; affected parties need to know what and by when.
- In capture cases, publishing the timeline or criterion **is** the operative remedy.
- Against oral pressure: record the instruction or seek written confirmation; record your own reasoned position on the same file; refer up if it persists; continue the substantive duty meanwhile unless unsafe or unlawful.
- A verbal refusal leaves no trace and moves the pressure to a successor.
- Write briefly, factually and at the time; a retrospective argumentative note is weak evidence.
- The same documentation discipline makes a defensible file note for any discretionary decision — and it is the sequence that Mission Karmayogi's case-based and simulation learning on the iGOT-Karmayogi platform rewards, so the architecture is competency-building as well as exam technique.

## 12. Residual risk
- Name the specific risk; state the monitoring or review mechanism; name the accountable office.
- Assign monitoring away from whoever has an incentive to find nothing.
- Three sources: the risk created by the remedy itself; the risk the decision cannot reach; the risk of recurrence, which is where preventive institutional reform belongs.
- One or two risks, not five; do not invent one to fill the slot.
- If the residual risk is large enough to change the decision, revisit the decision rather than confessing it in the last line.

## 13. The dissent ladder and the resignation test
- Four rungs: internal reporting on file with evidence; escalation to a higher or independent internal authority; statutory or lawful external channels; public disclosure as a genuine last resort.
- Compromised-rung rule: skip the captured rung, not the whole ladder.
- The Whistle Blowers Protection Act, 2014 (assent 9 May 2014) has never been brought into force and the 2015 amendment bill lapsed, so comprehensive statutory protection does not operate.
- Resignation test: are lawful internal options genuinely exhausted; would resigning improve the public-interest outcome or merely remove a principled voice; is staying with recorded dissent the more effective safeguard?
- Where the answer is to stay, name the contingency that would change it.

## 14. The consent-validity test
- Competence; authority; voluntariness; adequate information; scope; the emergency exception on **both** limbs; prospective consent design.
- A competent adult's consent cannot be given by another; a signature without authority is a record, not a consent.
- Scope is bounded in kind, extent and duration — diagnostic consent does not reach therapeutic surgery; eligibility consent does not reach surveillance.
- The emergency exception needs an immediate threat to life or health **and** unreasonableness in waiting.
- Prospective design supplies the systemic fix and the residual-risk close.
- Good intention does not supply missing authority; documentation does not supply missing validity.

## 15. The automated adverse-decision template
- Never auto-final; notice with an intelligible reason; access to the record; alternative evidence or cascade; human reconsideration by a named person with recorded reasons; reasoned outcome with timed appeal; published error-rate or disparate-impact audit.
- Record access is the sharpest question — without it an error cannot be corrected and "no proof of bias" rewards the opacity.
- The cascade must be designed for the **known failure population**.
- Structure the exception — do not abolish it and do not leave it free; the difference from discretion is the audit trail.
- Automation bias converts a device's uncertainty into administrative certainty; accountability cannot be delegated to the algorithm.

## 16. The parallel two-track template
- Continuity track runs publicly from day one; accountability track runs confidentially from day one; they are simultaneous, not sequential.
- Continuity instruments: published disposal sequence and criteria; dated pendency dashboard; single-window receipt with dated acknowledgment; random or committee-based allocation; fixed timelines with escalation; a signed published periodic report.
- Accountability instruments: secret preliminary verification; targeted file audit; referral with any required prior approval; a protected reporting channel; action confined to those against whom material exists.
- Wrongdoing of this kind is rent on opacity and delay — publishing the queue destroys the rent faster than prosecution.
- Each track is the other's safeguard. Announce the **new process**, not the investigation.
- Repair the specific captured element rather than abandoning a statutory mechanism.
- Brief staff and the employees' union early, framing the reform as protection of honest employees and of the office's reputation rather than as a collective accusation; keep verification correspondence outside ordinary circulation and name no official before the material supports it.
- Expect the union to reframe evidence-based action as victimisation — keep suspension criteria written, narrow and identical for everyone, and let the visible fairness of the process reform carry the rest.
- Publish an outcome summary at the end, so the public learns the official route worked.

## 17. Triage and criteria-based denial
- Triage: publish criteria in advance; state the ethical trade-off explicitly; record what was applied and by whom; review and adjust.
- The equity objection is real — build an explicit equity review into the criteria and audit rather than returning to discretion.
- Ownership does not license a different triage ethic under shared regulatory oversight; internal resourcing remains the private entity's prerogative.
- Denial template: pre-published criteria fixed before claimants are known; screens before commercial factors; claimant-by-claimant documentation; denial in process terms; a conditional future channel; any override in writing; conditions built into the instrument.
- Criteria written after the claimants appear are indistinguishable from rationalisation; a screen weighed against revenue has ceased to be a screen.
- Published criteria are judgement exercised in advance, when it was at its best.

## 18. Disaster and personal duty
- The case where no option is illegal — weighted checks do all the work.
- Facts: more than 200 dead and about 5,000 injured; roads and telecommunications disrupted; mother dies in another state; only close relative abroad; rain resumes after five days.
- Four options including remote management; decision is a short, written, monitored delegation with attendance at essential rites.
- Steelman: any command gap is unacceptable — met by converting it into a design requirement of check-in schedule and authority limits.
- Residual risk: the deputy's execution risk, mitigated by a short absence, check-ins and an after-action review owned by the Deputy Commissioner personally.
- Transferable rule: ask whether the public duty can be **discharged differently** rather than abandoned — and say so honestly where the facts close that route.

## 19. Financial propriety and procurement conflict
- Two failure types: sanction and delegation evasion; and a private stake in a decision. Both hard thresholds.
- Split work order: name the propriety principle, verify the works provision rather than quoting the goods-specific bar, and use the genuine emergency provision rather than artificial splitting.
- Decision: a technically independent emergency stabilisation within delegated power with reasons recorded, plus same-day referral of the balance. The contractor does not structure the paperwork.
- Residual risk: phasing misread as a disguised split — recorded technical justification, countersigned rationale, internal-audit review before closure.
- Brother's bid: disclosure on file before bid opening is finalised; alternate briefed only on published criteria; recusal extended to future tenders.
- Privileged information: request and refusal on file; published committee-based criteria; and the escalation to undue advantage if acted on.
- Family thread: the defence offered is always the actor's own reliability.

## 20. Public money, resignation and the compromised rung
- Diagnostic: is the action illegal, or improper but arguably procedurally valid? Where merely improper, legality eliminates nothing.
- Welfare re-appropriation: written reasoned objection; written confirmation sought; escalation to financial and then audit or parliamentary oversight; resignation on a named compulsion contingency only.
- Residual risk: escalation overruled by the same functionaries — ensure the record independently reaches the audit trail.
- Captured first rung: legality **is** a hard threshold; skip the compromised rung to the independent council, then the regulator within a defined window.
- Record the original result and the instruction before acting further; seek written confirmation of the instruction.
- Keeping records outside custody is defensible only if scoped to the specific result and instruction.

## 21. Political pressure and neutrality
- Two sources of pressure, and the second looks like an ally.
- Decision: document irregularities; seek written clarification; escalate through audit and vigilance and the overseeing department; decline the partisan channel politely and on record.
- Symmetry test: would this act be proper if the same request came from the other political side?
- Steelman: slow channels look like complicity — met by a time-bound escalation plus safeguarding decisions within his own authority in the interim.
- Residual risk: slow-walking and retaliatory transfer — the record reaches the oversight body independently, and a dated personal record of pressure is reviewed outside the management chain.
- The method guarantees auditable conduct and an existing record, not the outcome.

## 22. Public order and compromised administration
- Distinguish the short-horizon rumour event from the protracted conflict with a compromised administration.
- Rumour event: urgent verification; leader dialogue; a scaled-down, rerouted, supervised assembly; a written, reasoned, time-bound restriction; police visible at the market rather than at the procession.
- Protracted conflict: the instrument the DM would normally use is the broken one, so every action solves for the tool first.
- Name the issues precisely, including that impartiality is neither identical treatment nor equidistance while identical relief norms are required because need is the criterion.
- Five-stage sequence: life-preservation by neutral escorted convoys on published equal per-capita norms; reconstitution on written conduct-based non-prejudicial criteria; visible neutrality through a single spokesperson and symmetric registers; a joint peace committee with a published agenda and timeline; and time-bound, narrowly tailored, written, published, periodically reviewed restrictions.
- Reconciling principle: non-partisanship is symmetry of **process and criteria**, not of outcome.
- A post-facto commission of inquiry is an accountability instrument, not a live-crisis tool; verify renumbered section numbers before citing.

## 23. Consent and research integrity
- Both cases: an authority belonging to someone else exercised by someone who believed they knew better.
- Therapeutic consent: information and scope fail, and the emergency exception fails on both limbs.
- Named anchor's holdings: consent must be real and valid; diagnostic consent does not extend to therapeutic surgery absent necessity plus unreasonable delay; no question arises of another consenting for a competent adult.
- Strong paternalism, not weak; therapeutic privilege does not apply because the patient was never asked.
- Conduct appraisal separates motive, rightness of the act, and conduct after the fact — verdict: not malicious, but not defensible.
- Systemic fix: pre-operative extended-procedure discussion; a written policy on who may consent; frozen-section and second-opinion protocols; consent audit owned by the ethics committee.
- Research case: the ethics committee is the untainted authority, not the scientist or the board; re-consent through an independent counsellor with a follow-up site audit.
- Cite the principle and the named judgment; verify current instruments and section numbers.

## 24. Harassment and custodial due process
- A due-process mechanism protects complainant **and** respondent; failing either destroys the other.
- Statutory requirements: a committee where ten or more are employed; at least half its members women; an external member; written complaint within three months, extendable; inquiry within ninety days; employer action within sixty.
- The compromised element is the presiding officer, not the law — repair the committee, do not abandon the mechanism.
- Custodial case hinge: the missing arrest memo, medical examination and custody-diary entries are a **certain** breach independent of the contested force question.
- Safeguards: attested arrest memo countersigned by the arrestee; information to a friend or relative; medical examination at arrest and at roughly forty-eight-hour intervals with injuries recorded; case diary and custody register; information to the magistrate.
- Steelman: an immediate inquiry presumes guilt — met by the procedural obligation owed regardless of the finding and by the protection an independent inquiry gives an innocent officer.
- The workplace or departmental inquiry runs alongside, not instead of, any criminal process.

## 25. Humanitarian obligation at the border
- The trap is a uniform response to a mixed group; both blanket answers fail for the same reason.
- "Appears to be a foreign uniform" is an information constraint — a status to be determined.
- India is not a party to the 1951 Convention or the 1967 Protocol and has no dedicated refugee statute; the ordinary foreigners-law procedure governs status.
- The reasoning on the right to remain came in an **interim order declining a stay**, not a final judgment; the customary-law non-refoulement question remains open.
- Articles 14 and 21 apply to every person on Indian soil including non-citizens, on a final judgment.
- Decision: separate immediately — disarm and refer the armed stream for screening under the ordinary law; shelter, treat and register civilians pending case-by-case determination; force only as necessary and proportionate.
- The separation principle generalises to any group an official is urged to treat uniformly.
- Residual risk: misclassification — documented reviewable per-individual records, a defined review window, central escalation. The remedy is reviewability, not confidence.

## 26. Technology as decision-maker
- Data sharing: proportionality — legitimate aim, necessity, proportionate means-end balance — plus purpose limitation; urgency is not a legal basis.
- Decision: a specific written reasoned order identifying necessity and a narrow dataset; data minimisation; logged disclosure; oral political pressure converted into a written record.
- Predictive policing: name the eight issues, with feedback-loop bias decisive because arrest records are a record of past **enforcement**, not past crime.
- The burden of proof runs the wrong way; the single decisive reason is that coercive action against an identified individual must rest on evidence she can see and contest.
- Decision: suspend person-level targeting and crowd-biometric matching, retain place-based deployment, time-bound independent audit questioning the arrest baseline, human-in-the-loop with recorded reasons, notice and correction, review of detentions already made, a published district policy, a community-liaison mechanism.
- Audit trap: it may run on the same contaminated data — independent auditors, victimisation-survey benchmarking, a named accountable officer, a second review; and monitor displacement.
- Welfare gate: a leaked quintal is recoverable, a missed month is not — the errors are asymmetric, and fiscal probity means preventing leakage rather than minimising disbursal.
- Decision: a cascade ending in a numbered, photographed, countersigned, auto-syncing exception register with a rate threshold flagging outlier shops, plus doorstep or nominee collection, portability, shop display, a timed grievance route, monthly social audit and offline-capable devices.
- Thesis: evidence-aid versus eligibility-determinant, grounded in burden allocation, non-random error distribution and accountability displacement; the technology is not the ethical variable, the fallback is.

## 27. Environment, corporate duty and international transfer
- The family's capacity: taking seriously an interest with no representative in the room.
- Forest case: welfare is not an automatic override; hideout and safety claims require evidence; the decision is a time-bound alternatives comparison with independent ecological and social verification; the residual risk runs both ways.
- Corporate emissions: a pledge is not verified implementation; offsets without changed demand shift responsibility; a penalty needs transparent criteria, due process and equal treatment; rebound demand is the named residual risk.
- Export case: name supplier complicity, diversion and non-state-actor risk, proliferation posture, the recipient-population question, replaceability, legitimate self-defence supply, and staged documented due diligence.
- Support to guerrilla forces abroad is dispositive before any weighing of revenue.
- Sequencing rule: threshold screens before commercial comparison.
- Rebut replaceability on four grounds — moral, proves-too-much, supplier reputation, and leverage over the wrong variable.
- Thesis: the interests are time-horizon-dependent; build ethics into the instrument, pre-publish the criteria, and put any political override in writing.

## 28. Writing the answer
- Roughly 120-130 words per subpart; elements are not equal in size.
- Signpost rather than narrate; name rather than describe.
- The eight-move spine: separate facts from assumptions; name stakeholders including one silent; name the binding constraint; generate three to four options; thresholds then weighted checks then the decisive one; decide executably with the objection answered; implement and communicate to three audiences; close with risk, mechanism and owner.
- Three patterns in the 2024-2026 bank: a named legal anchor to supply unprompted; technology-mediated administrative harm; and the protagonist's own institution as the compromised instrument.
- Standing citation cautions: works-procurement rather than the goods bar; renumbered criminal-procedure sections; superseded medical-conduct and penal good-faith provisions; the current welfare entitlement provision; India's regime-by-regime export-control status.
- State the principle and name the instrument rather than guessing a section number.
- Final thesis: Section B tests not whether you know what is right but whether you can reach it in a way another official could audit.
- The architecture is transferable beyond the exam: it is how a defensible file note for a discretionary decision is written, and it is the sequence that Mission Karmayogi's case-based and simulation learning on the iGOT-Karmayogi platform (Cabinet approval 2 September 2020) is designed to build. Verify any specific named module before citing one as a current, programme-wide requirement.

## Official live sources used only as dated verification anchors

- Whistle Blowers Protection Act, 2014 commencement status — live-verified October 2026 across a Press Information Bureau release, a Lok Sabha question annexure and corroborating national reporting: the Act received assent on 9 May 2014, has not been brought into force, and the 2015 amendment bill lapsed. Used only as the status fact that sets the risk calculus at the dissent ladder's final rung; the statutory architecture itself is owned by the corruption-law topic.
- India's position on the 1951 Refugee Convention and its 1967 Protocol — live-verified October 2026: India is not a party to either and has no dedicated refugee statute, and the question whether customary international law binds India to non-refoulement independent of treaty membership remains an open legal debate. Used only to state the constraint accurately and to record the open question as open.
- Succession and renumbering of criminal procedure provisions — live-verified October 2026: the custodial safeguards established by the leading reported judgment were given statutory force through the arrest-procedure provisions and are carried forward under the 2023 successor code, which renumbered the former provisions. Used only to justify citing the safeguards by content while deliberately withholding section numbers.
- Succession of medical professional-conduct regulation and penal good-faith provisions — live-verified October 2026: the professional-conduct regime and the penal code have both been replaced, so the regulations on written consent before operations and the good-faith provisions have been superseded or renumbered. Used only to support the standing citation caution.
- The administrative position on authentication failure in welfare delivery — live-verified October 2026: authentication failure is not a ground for denying a benefit, and alternate viable means of identification are required. Used only as the entitlement floor; the current statutory provision and departmental instruction were not confirmed section-by-section and are deliberately not cited.
- Structured, case-based and simulation learning in Indian civil-services capacity building — live-verified October 2026 as a continuing practice under the national capacity-building programme's online platform. Used only to note that the same structured sequence is practised institutionally; no specific current module is named.

## Open uncertainty retained rather than silently resolved

- The exact printed stems of the 2018-2025 GS-IV Section-B cases are not held in the local verified ledgers, which record neutral renderings only. Those forty-eight rows are therefore labelled paraphrase-labelled neutral routing and are not quoted, and no stem has been reconstructed from memory, aggregator material or coaching sources. The twelve 2026 subparts are quoted because the local supplement records them as read from the official-paper text.
- The 130 to 120 split between Sections A and B is a three-year verified pattern across 2024, 2025 and 2026 rather than a permanently guaranteed weightage; this session states it as such and directs verification against the current year's own paper.
- The precise works-procurement provision and delegation-of-financial-powers instrument governing the expenditure-splitting case were not independently confirmed in this pass. Consistent with the canonical owner's own scope caution, this session names the propriety principle and expressly declines to quote the goods-specific piecemeal-purchase bar as if it were the works citation.
- The current section numbers for custodial safeguards, prohibitory-order and unlawful-assembly powers under the 2023 successor criminal procedure code were not independently confirmed. This session teaches the safeguards and the restriction standard by content and deliberately does not cite section numbers.
- The current instrument and section numbers for professional-conduct regulation of written consent, and for the penal good-faith provisions, were not independently confirmed. This session cites the principle and the named judgment only.
- The current statutory provision and departmental instruction establishing that authentication failure is not a ground for denying a welfare benefit were not confirmed section-by-section. The settled principle is stated and the citation is withheld.
- India's membership or party status across individual export-control regimes, and the specific lists, authorisation routes and regime thresholds, were not independently verified. Consistent with the canonical owner's caution, this session names the instruments by principle — end-user certification, no-re-transfer undertakings, post-shipment verification, the diversion bar — and does not assert any status, list or threshold.
- The constitutional question whether customary international law binds India to non-refoulement independent of treaty membership remains unresolved, and the judicial reasoning on the right to remain arose in an interim order declining a stay rather than in a final judgment. This session states both facts and resolves neither.
- The specific current modules of the national capacity-building programme's case-based and simulation learning were not independently confirmed. This session notes the practice and names no module, consistent with the canonical owner's own verification caution.
